  - An update refused while a daemon runs.
  - `other-root` and `unrecognized` refusals that leave the file byte-identical.
  - A failure injected at each step, with the `install_rolled_back` or `install_incomplete` result and its leftover list.
  - An uninstall that waits with no deadline for a held drain, and stop-wins during that wait.
  - Deterministic fake-manager interleavings of uninstall with a crash relaunch: before, during, and after step 2, and between step 3's last observation and step 4. In every one, no process holding the lock or accepted work receives a signal, and a process launched after step 2 exits 0 before the lock.
  - One test per *Outcomes* row on each platform it applies to, asserting the result, the exit code, the bytes written back or not, the registration after, the ordered manager calls, and the retained identity. That includes the successful uninstall from `ours`. After macOS `unload-unknown`, including a failed `launchctl print` after `bootout`, and after `stop_unconfirmed`, no row writes bytes back, and no row issues a second unload or stop in that invocation. Linux and Windows `unload-failed` rows and a successful rollback write back exactly the bytes their rows name.
  - The stop-accepted, reply-lost trace from Gate M, repeated for uninstall from `ours` on all three platforms and for resumed uninstall and rollback on Linux against MainPID.
  - A no-lock job pid that is before its registration check, and one that read `ours` before withdrawal but has not taken the lock. In both, uninstall from `ours` waits, then stops through `runStop`.
  - A manager pid query that errors, times out, or returns unparsable output before step 4. Uninstall never runs step 4, writes the kept bytes back, and names the error.
  - The Gate M pid-reuse and resumed-uninstall traces repeated on Linux against MainPID, and on Windows the `job-only` rule that never stops a process.
  - A foreign manager pid that is never signaled.
  - An interrupted uninstall that resumes.
  - Convergence on rerun.
- [ ] `service-launch.test.ts` covers `daemon start` routed through the manager, with no detached fallback when the manager fails, the port mismatch refusal, the foreground `daemon run` refusal and its `--bot` exemption, and the exit code for each case in *Exit codes*. That includes 75 only when the restart intent survives, 0 under stop-wins, and 0 before the lock when the registration is not `ours`.
  - The real call chain of the *Pre-lock guard*. These cases drive `runHostRuntime` itself, the function that `cli/index.ts`'s `daemon run` action calls, with `config/data-access` and `server/pid-lock` module-mocked to record calls. A standalone guard function does not satisfy them.
    - `VONVON_SERVICE` set and the registration `absent`. Neither `acquireMutex` nor `acquirePidLock` is called, `runRun` is not imported, and the exit code is 0.
    - A live owner already present. Neither lock function is called, and the owner's `daemon.pid` bytes are unchanged. `releasePidLock` and `closeMutex` are not called, and the exit code is 0.
    - A collision found only at `acquirePidLock`, which throws while another live owner holds the lock. The exit code is 0, and `releasePidLock` is not called for that owner.
    - `process.send` present, the supervisor's Bot child. The guard reads nothing and V3's path is unchanged.
    - No `VONVON_SERVICE` and the registration `ours`. This is the foreground refusal, with exit 1 and no lock.
  - One subprocess case runs the built CLI, `node <entry> daemon run --port <P>`, with `VONVON_SERVICE` set for the host platform. It uses a temporary `HOME` and `VONVON_HOME` that have no registration. It asserts exit 0 and that no `daemon.pid` and no host mutex file were created. A second subprocess case writes an `ours` definition and a `daemon.pid` naming the live test-runner process. It asserts exit 0 and that the `daemon.pid` bytes are unchanged.
  - Managed core update, with a fake manager. The helper receives the exact `restarting` record and marks it `done` only after an authenticated same-port replacement. It writes `error` on `superseded` or `start_failed`. It writes nothing when the record changed or `update.lock` is held.
  - A stop before the release step cancels the helper, writes `error`, and exits 0 with no relaunch. A `helper_lost` writes `error` and exits 0.
  - A plain managed restart spawns no helper and leaves a stale `restarting` record untouched. The replacement never writes the record.
  - On macOS and Windows, the managed-update helper is spawned with `detached: true`.
  - On Linux, Gate L's deterministic tests.
  Run `npm test -w packages/daemon -- service-`.

**Verify, live.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A PR is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked. Ten lanes on the configured `swarm workers` model at the PR head, per the boot recipe.

- [ ] Lane 1. Regression lane against trunk. Launch the old app at `origin/codex/device-handoff` and the new shell at head with the same fixtures. Save `launch.png`. Pass when both windows show the Bot list.
- [ ] Lane 2. Launch with no daemon running. Save `cold.png`. Pass when the shell starts the bundled daemon and shows the list.
- [ ] Lane 3. Start the daemon from the CLI, then launch the shell. Save `attach.png`. Pass when the shell attaches and no second daemon exists.
- [ ] Lane 4. Close the window. Save `closed.txt`. Pass when the daemon and bots keep running.
- [ ] Lane 5. Launch the app twice. Save `second.png`. Pass when the existing window comes forward and no second app process remains.
- [ ] Lane 6. As the *Service gate*'s test user, which on the Mac may be `clay` under the rules in *Native acceptance environment*, turn on **Open at login**. Save `login-item.txt`. Pass when `launchctl print gui/$(id -u)/com.vonvon.bot.daemon` shows the job loaded. Repeat the lane on Linux, in a VM or host with a systemd user session and never in hosted CI. The app offers no login toggle on Linux, per *Service gate*, so there the lane runs `vonvon service install`, and `systemctl --user is-enabled vonvon-bot.service` must report enabled. Then turn **Open at login** off on the Mac and pass when the job is gone, the daemon drained and stopped, and the shell started a self-hosted daemon.
- [ ] Lane 7. Quit from the tray menu with **Open at login** on. Save `quit.txt`. Pass when the app exits and the daemon keeps running.
- [ ] Lane 8. Start an older daemon build, then launch the shell. Save `mismatch.png`. Pass when the shell shows the version prompt instead of a blank window.
- [ ] Lane 9. Bind a group from the desktop window. Save `bind.png`. Pass when the group appears and `projects.json` agrees.
- [ ] Lane 10. Check for updates against a local feed. Save `update.png`. Pass when the update panel shows the feed version and waits for the user's click.
- [ ] Native service acceptance per *Service gate*, in addition to the ten lanes and never in place of one. It runs on launchd on the Mac and on systemd on Linux, as the test user from *Native acceptance environment*, with a fresh `VONVON_HOME`. On Linux that is a dedicated user with its own uid and home, never `ops`. On the Mac it is a dedicated user or, under the user's override, `clay` with every rule listed there. It never runs in hosted CI, and no CI job or mock replaces it. If `vonvon-accept`, its systemd user session, or a passing containment read-back is missing, the systemd half stays unchecked, and V5 waits for the root's preparation under [native-acceptance-hosts.md](native-acceptance-hosts.md). Use the packaged build of the frozen head. On Linux that is the `global-npm` distribution, packed and installed on the host from a clone at the frozen head, per *Packaged build of the frozen head* in [native-acceptance-hosts.md](native-acceptance-hosts.md). A clone build never replaces it. `native-systemd.txt` begins with record B0, ends with the final record B, and holds every checkpoint record between them. Save `native-launchd.txt` and `native-systemd.txt`, and in each record every command, its exit code, and the pid, port, and manager state it observed.
  1. Before anything else, from the operator's account and read-only, record the bridge's registration (`launchctl print gui/<operator uid>/ai.feishu-codex-bridge.bot`, or `systemctl --user cat feishu-codex-bridge.service` and its `is-enabled` and `is-active`), its definition file's checksum, the checksums of the bridge data files that `control.ts`'s `installationPaths` names, and the bridge process ids with their start times. On the Linux host no bridge runs for V5. There step 1 records that `feishu-codex-bridge.service` is `not-found` for `vonvon-accept`, and takes record B0.
  2. `install` with no daemon running. The manager starts one on port `P`, and the manager's pid equals the lock owner's pid. Run `install` again and see `already installed`. Run `install` from a second `VONVON_HOME` and see a refusal, with the definition's checksum unchanged.
  3. `daemon start` attaches with no second process. `daemon start --port <other>` refuses. A foreground `daemon run` refuses.
  4. Crash. `kill -9` the daemon. A new pid owns the lock on port `P` within 15 s.
  5. Restart. Send `restartHost` through the local credential. The old pid exits 75, and a new pid owns port `P`.
  6. Unlimited drain. Hold the drain fixture from V3's CLI evidence bundle for at least 120 s, longer than launchd's and systemd's default stop timeouts. Run `vonvon daemon stop`. The daemon stays alive, and no `SIGKILL` arrives, until the fixture is released. Then it exits 0 and is not relaunched within 60 s. The registration is still present.
  7. Stop wins. Hold the fixture, send `restartHost`, then run `daemon stop` before releasing it. After the release the daemon exits 0 and nothing relaunches.
  8. Start after stop. `daemon start` starts the manager's job on port `P`.
  9. Login load. With the daemon stopped, unload and load the job the way a login does (`bootout` then `bootstrap`, or `daemon-reload` then `start` of the enabled unit). It starts on port `P`.
  10. Uninstall while the fixture is held. It waits, then removes the job and the definition. `launchctl print` or `systemctl --user cat` no longer finds it, no daemon runs, and a second `uninstall` reports `not installed`.
  10a. Resumed uninstall and A1. `install`, then `daemon stop`, so the job stays loaded with no pid. Remove the definition by hand, leaving the job loaded or the unit loaded, and start a self-hosted daemon with `daemon start`. Run `uninstall`. Pass when it returns `uninstalled`, prints the self-hosted pid, removes the job, and the self-hosted daemon keeps its pid and `startedAt`. Then repeat with the job's own daemon running when the definition is removed: pass when it is stopped through `stopHost` after its drain and the job is removed. Across steps 2 to 10a, every manager pid query taken while a manager child held the lock equals the lock's pid, and none reports a pid after that child exited. That is the native check of A1.
  11. Failure. Make the agent or unit directory unwritable, or in `clay` place a run-owned read-only file at the product's plist path, and run `install`. It reports `install_refused` or `install_rolled_back` and leaves nothing behind. Restore the directory.
  12. Repeat step 1. Pass only when every step held and the bridge records are identical. In `clay` that includes the bridge pid's start time and listeners. Any difference fails the box. On the Linux host, step 12 runs owned teardown T and then the final record B, and every compared field must equal B0.

**Verify, perf.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A PR is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked.

- [ ] Metric. Time from app launch to the Bot list painted, cold and with a running daemon, and total RSS of app plus daemon.
- [ ] Probe. Interleave five launches of the old app and five of the new shell, timed from process start to the first paint event.
- [ ] Baseline. Record the old app numbers first.
- [ ] Rule. Fail if the new shell is more than 15% slower or uses more than 15% more memory.

**Review evidence.** Keep the prescribed captures and independent verdict before guarded Shipping.

- [ ] Copy lane 1, 2, and 8 screenshots into `~/vonvon-review/V5-review-<slug>.png`. Include the Linux transcript from lane 6.
- [ ] Record a 30 to 60 second video of the change in a lane worktree. Save it as `~/vonvon-review/V5-review.mp4`.
- [ ] Save the screenshots and video with the PR evidence. Codex proceeds after the independent exact-head Shipping verdict; personal review is optional.

**Merge.**

- [ ] Root's clean verdict at the exact head SHA.
- [ ] Bugbot triage done.
- [ ] Base and verdict are current under the execution playbook and the patch-id rule in `playbooks/shipping.md`.
- [ ] Codex merges V5 only after the exact-head independent Shipping verdict is clean.

## Replace the Claude Code backend with the Paseo provider (V6)

**Depends on.** V3. Development starts from `H3`. Acceptance needs the Feishu fixture Bot and the real Claude Code CLI. V6 merges after V5, in roadmap order. V6 publishes the freeze point `F6`.

**Files.**

- [ ] Create `packages/daemon/src/agent/paseo/` with `agent-sdk-types.ts`, `session-backend.ts`, `closure.json`, `logger.ts`, and the import closure below.
- [ ] Create `packages/daemon/src/agent/providers/` with `claude/` and the shared provider helpers below.
- [ ] Create `packages/daemon/src/agent/paseo-claude-wrapper.ts` for the wrapper repairs under *Copied-source exceptions*.
- [ ] Create `packages/daemon/test/session-backend.test.ts`, `paseo-closure.test.ts`, `claude-permission.test.ts`, `claude-sdk-boundary.test.ts`, and the ported Paseo tests under `packages/daemon/test/paseo/`.
- [ ] Edit `packages/daemon/src/agent/index.ts`, `agent/catalog.ts`, which the registry treats as authoritative, and `NOTICE`. The root checks the `createBackend('claude-agent')` lookup in `server/snapshot.ts`, per *Shared files*.
- [ ] Delete `packages/daemon/src/agent/claude-agent/`.

*Import closure.* A copy of `providers/claude/` alone does not compile. `agent.ts` and its helper tree import about 20 other Paseo modules, about 40 type names from `agent-sdk-types.ts`, and several `@getpaseo/protocol` subpaths. The rule is to copy whole modules and change only their import specifiers. The source is Paseo `0009a7e71`, and its paths are relative to `packages/server/src/server/`.

- Compute the closure first. Starting from `agent/agent-sdk-types.ts` and `agent/providers/claude/agent.ts`, follow every static import, every dynamic `import()`, and every `import type` to its file. Stop at npm packages. Write the result to `agent/paseo/closure.json`. Each entry has the source path, the destination path, the source file's SHA-256 at the pin, and its modification kind: `specifiers` or `adapted`, with a reason. Codex's closure count at `H3` (47 files, 26 of them outside `claude/`) is a check on the result, not a substitute for it.
- Destinations.
  - `agent/providers/**` goes to `packages/daemon/src/agent/providers/**`, keeping the relative path. This holds `claude/**`, `provider-runner`, `diagnostic-utils`, and `provider-image-output`.
  - Every other closure file under `agent/` goes to `packages/daemon/src/agent/paseo/`, keeping its path relative to `agent/`. This holds `agent-sdk-types`, `provider-options`, `prompt-attachments`, `provider-history-timestamps`, `provider-notices`, `provider-session-import`, `provider-refresh-deadline`, `provider-launch-config`, `system-prompt`, `tools/types`, and `executable-resolution/**`.
  - Closure files outside `agent/` go to `agent/paseo/runtime/`, keeping their path relative to `packages/server/src/`. This holds `server/paseo-env` and `utils/{spawn,tree-kill,promise-timeout}`.
  - The process launcher. Pinned `utils/spawn.ts` imports `spawnProcess` and `execCommand` from `@getpaseo/plugin/server`. Copy the two plugin files that implement them, `packages/plugin/src/server/process.ts` and `packages/plugin/src/server/process-internal/windows-command.ts`, to `agent/paseo/plugin/server/process.ts` and `agent/paseo/plugin/server/process-internal/windows-command.ts`, whole, with Paseo's Apache-2.0 notice. In `runtime/utils/spawn.ts`, the specifier `@getpaseo/plugin/server` changes to `../../plugin/server/process.js`. That is a `specifiers` change in `closure.json`, and the plugin files keep their own specifiers. The Windows `.cmd` and `.bat` shell selection and the argument quoting in `windows-command.ts` stay byte-for-byte. No other `@getpaseo/plugin` module is copied, and the launcher is not replaced by `node:child_process`, `cross-spawn`, or the old bridge's `platform/spawn`. If the closure computation finds another `@getpaseo/plugin` import, it is a blocker, per the rule below.
  - Every `@getpaseo/protocol` subpath the closure imports, such as `agent-types`, `messages`, `provider-config`, `forge-manifest`, `tool-name-normalization`, and the tool-call display module, is copied from Paseo's protocol package into `agent/paseo/protocol/`, along with its own closure. It does not go into `packages/protocol`, whose shapes stay unchanged.
- Removals, recorded in `closure.json` with a reason.
  - The Paseo tool catalog runtime, meaning `tools/` other than `tools/types.ts`, and the tool policy and serialization modules. An import of these is deleted together with the code that offers Paseo's own tools to Claude. Vonvon has no Paseo tool catalog.
  - The subagent store runtime, `provider-subagents/store.ts`, and the timeline store it imports. The type declarations the closure needs from it, such as `ProviderSubagentInputEvent` and `ProviderSubagentStatus`, are copied verbatim into `agent/paseo/provider-subagents/types.ts`, together with any type they reference. The `claude/subagents/**` sources stay, because they are part of the provider.
  - `pino`. Its only use is `import type { Logger }`. The specifier changes to the new `agent/paseo/logger.ts`, which declares the structural `Logger` interface the closure calls. `session-backend.ts` adapts the daemon's logger to it.
  - No other module is replaced, and nothing is filled in from the old `claude-agent/` or written from scratch. An import that cannot be resolved under these rules is a blocker. Name it in the PR and hand it back to Claude Code, and keep working on everything else.
- *Copied-source exceptions.* Exactly one logic change is allowed in copied bytes: settings sources. Every other V3 behavior the copy lacks is restored in Vonvon-owned wrapper code, and the copied files stay pinned.
  - Settings sources. Pinned `providers/claude/agent.ts` declares the constant `CLAUDE_SETTING_SOURCES` as `['user', 'project', 'local']`, and the one base-options object built in `buildOptions` uses it, with the session binding (`sessionId` for a new session, `resume` for a resumed one) spread into that same object. V3's `claude-agent` passes `['user', 'project']`, and no `ClaudeProviderOptionsSchema` field overrides it. The only edit is removing the `'local'` element from that constant, which covers new and resumed sessions together.
    - There is no new option, setting, environment variable, wire field, or storage field, and no way to turn `local` back on.
    - In `closure.json`, `agent.ts` is `adapted`, with the symbol `CLAUDE_SETTING_SOURCES` and the reason "settingSources parity with V3". It records the pinned source SHA-256, `41351a3c995142ae296567637c7611e24305579b4694947d0b685a50f284ef2f` per the review, and the integrated SHA-256 recomputed after this edit. The integrated hash is not the pinned one, and `f1142c6897323c9bdba6a6930a0d8ce441ebcf0a4af172d45cab8a7293efa98a`, the current branch file, is neither until the item below is settled. The modification notice at the top of the file names exactly the specifier changes, the structural logger type, and this constant, and `NOTICE` covers it.
    - The current branch notice also lists a "strict slash-command item guard". The delta is one line: pinned `providers/claude/agent.ts:3523` is `content.push(slashCommand);`, and the integrated file at `:3524` is `if (slashCommand) content.push(slashCommand);`. That is a logic change this plan never approved. Codex either restores the pinned line, or keeps it only with a test that shows the two lines push the same `content` for every value `slashCommand` can hold at that site at runtime, including any falsy value the code before it can produce, and records that proof in `closure.json`. Calling it a type-only change is not enough, because the guard is a runtime branch. If neither is done, or the values differ, the guard is a blocker returned to Claude Code.
    - If the constant is not the only source of `settingSources` in the copied tree, that is a blocker. It does not justify a broader edit.
  - Wrapper repairs. These are in `session-backend.ts` and a new `agent/paseo-claude-wrapper.ts`, which Vonvon owns and which carry no Paseo header. They change no copied byte, no assertion, and no wire shape, and they do not depend on V5.
    - Raw SDK observer. `session-backend.ts` passes the provider's existing injectable `queryFactory` option, for both `startSession` and `resumeSession`. The factory calls the SDK's `query` and returns a `Proxy` over the returned `Query` that delegates every member to it, binding functions to the target, and copies each yielded `SDKMessage` to a per-turn observer before yielding it unchanged. It never alters, drops, reorders, or synthesizes a message.
    - Terminal API error then empty success (issue 65). When the observer sees a main-chain `assistant` message with an `error` field in the current turn, the wrapper turns that turn's `turn_completed` into V3's terminal error event, with V3's message text for that error kind, and emits no `done` for it. V3's existing API error matrix stays, and every row of it must pass through the copied provider.
    - Bash description. The wrapper keeps the raw `tool_use` input by tool-use id from the observer and fills the Bash tool call's `description` from it, as V3 shows it.
    - Usage. From the raw `result` message's `usage` and `modelUsage`, the wrapper reproduces V3's cache-token totals (input plus cache-creation plus cache-read) and V3's model context-window fallback, and reports them in place of the copied provider's usage.
    - Raw tool history (issue 66). The wrapper's `readHistory` and `listThreads` read the session's JSONL as V3 did: Read tool results with their content, array tool results flattened as V3 flattened them, the preview chosen as custom title, then summary, then first prompt, and the thread's creation time.
    - If a behavior cannot be rebuilt from the raw SDK messages or the session file without a copied-byte edit, that behavior is a blocker returned to Claude Code. It is never relabeled as accepted behavior.
- Each copied file keeps its Apache-2.0 header. Each changed file gets a modification notice, and `NOTICE` gets the Paseo section.
- Dependencies. The root moves `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` from `devDependencies` (`^0.3.178`) to `dependencies`, at exactly `0.3.246`, Paseo's pin. It does this in V6 only. The daemon's `zod` `4.6.5` satisfies Paseo's `^4.4.3`. Any other external package the closure imports, such as `tree-kill` or `which`, is added at Paseo's range, from `closure.json`.
- Tests. Copy every Paseo `*.test.ts` beside a copied file, except e2e and live tests, to the matching path under `packages/daemon/test/paseo/`, and change only their specifiers. A test that imports outside the closure is excluded, and `closure.json` lists it with its reason.

*Permission mapping.* Vonvon keeps V3's Claude permission behavior. It adds no interactive approval in Feishu and changes no wire shape.

- `session-backend.ts` passes each bridge mode to the copied provider. The four SDK fields the provider builds must equal what V3's `permissionOptions(mode, network, cwd)` in `claude-agent/permission.ts` returns, field for field: `permissionMode`, `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions`, `sandbox`, and `disallowedTools`.
  - An undefined mode is `full`, as V3's `mode ?? 'full'`.
  - `full`: `permissionMode: 'bypassPermissions'` and `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true`. There is no `sandbox` and no `disallowedTools`, whatever `network` is. V3 returns before it reads `network` for `full`.
  - `write`: the same two fields, plus `sandbox` `{enabled: true, failIfUnavailable: true, autoAllowBashIfSandboxed: true, allowUnsandboxedCommands: false}`.
  - `qa`: the `write` fields, with `sandbox.filesystem.denyWrite: [cwd]`, and `disallowedTools` starting with `Write`, `Edit`, and `NotebookEdit`.
  - Network for `qa` and `write`: when `network` is `false` or undefined (V3's `!network`), append `WebFetch` and `WebSearch` to `disallowedTools`. When it is `true`, nothing is appended. `write` with `network: true` therefore has no `disallowedTools` field at all, since V3 omits an empty list.
  - Where each field comes from. `permissionMode` comes from the `modeId` that `session-backend.ts` passes, `bypassPermissions`. `allowDangerouslySkipPermissions: true` is hardcoded by the pinned provider at `agent.ts:3384`, and `session-backend.ts` does not set it. `sandbox` and `disallowedTools` are passed as `providerOptions`, which `ClaudeProviderOptionsSchema` validates. The first two fields are not in that strict schema, and `session-backend.ts` never passes them there.
  - If the copied provider cannot produce one of these exact values through those inputs without changing provider logic, that field is a blocker. The lanes that depend on it, lane 4 or 5, stay gated, and Claude Code decides the fix.
- Any `permission_requested` that still reaches `session-backend.ts`, for example from `AskUserQuestion` or a tool outside the mode's pre-approved set, becomes the existing passive `approval_request` event with the request's id, title, and detail. It is then resolved at once through `AgentSession.respondToPermission` as a denial, with the message that Vonvon Bot does not take interactive approvals in this mode and that Claude should continue without that action. The mode's own rules are already pre-approved through the options above, so denying everything else reproduces V3. `capabilities.approvals` keeps the value V3's `claude-agent` declares.
- `abort` first denies any request still unresolved, then calls `interrupt`. A prompt arriving with a request pending cannot happen, because every request resolves when it arrives. `close` does the same before closing the session.
- `steer` maps to `steerActiveTurn`. Resume maps to the client's resume path with the stored provider session id. Models map to `fetchCatalog` without a turn. Compact maps to the session's compaction path, or to `/compact` if that is what V3's `claude-agent` sent. A missing binary maps from the provider's launch error to V3's not-installed error text. These mappings change no `AgentEvent` or `AgentThread` shape.
- Subagent-pane events. Paseo emits some stream and timeline events only for its dedicated subagent pane. Vonvon has no such pane and no wire event for it, so `session-backend.ts` ignores those events and maps nothing from them. The full `agent-sdk-types.ts` keeps every type, and the provider's `claude/subagents/**` bookkeeping runs unchanged. No subagent wire event and no Feishu approval feature is added.

**Build.**

- [ ] Compute and copy the closure per *Import closure*, writing `closure.json` first.
- [ ] `session-backend.ts` wraps any `AgentClient` as an `AgentBackend`. It maps timeline events to `AgentEvent`, `permission_requested` to `approval_request` with the resolution in *Permission mapping*, `steerActiveTurn` to `steer`, and `interrupt` to `abort`.
- [ ] Map the bridge modes `qa`, `write`, and `full` per *Permission mapping*.
- [ ] Register the provider in `agent/index.ts` and delete `claude-agent/`. The provider id `claude-agent` stays. The root may adjust existing fields of that entry in `agent/catalog.ts`, such as its kind, detection, or install metadata, only so that an installed Claude Code stays visible in the Feishu provider picker. It must check that `isBackendEntryInstalled` still reports it installed. At V3 that function returns `false` for every `external-cli` entry, so switching only the kind would hide Claude. No new id, field, or wire shape is added, and V7 is unchanged.

**You see.**

- [ ] A group bound to Claude Code answers in Feishu with a streaming run card, accepts a follow-up mid-turn, and stops within 2 s.

**Verify, unit.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A PR is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked.

- [ ] `packages/daemon/test/session-backend.test.ts` drives a fake `AgentSession` through text, tool call, permission, steer, interrupt, and error, and asserts the `AgentEvent` sequence. A permission yields `approval_request` and then a denial through `respondToPermission`. An abort with a pending request denies it before `interrupt`. A subagent-pane event produces no `AgentEvent`. Run `npm test -w packages/daemon -- session-backend`.
- [ ] `paseo-closure.test.ts` recomputes the import closure from the copied files. It fails on an unresolved import, on a file missing from `closure.json` or listed there but absent, and on any import of the removed tool catalog, the subagent store runtime, `pino`, or any `@getpaseo/*` specifier. After the launcher redirect, none remains. It also checks that the two copied plugin files match their pinned SHA-256 values, so the Windows quoting is unchanged.
- [ ] `claude-permission.test.ts` captures the SDK `Options` through the provider's injectable `queryFactory` for an undefined mode, `qa`, `write`, and `full`, each with `network` `true`, `false`, and undefined. It asserts the four fields against what V3's `permissionOptions` returns for the same inputs, including that `full` never has `disallowedTools`. For a new session and for a resumed session, the captured `settingSources` deep-equals `['user', 'project']`, and never contains `local`.
- [ ] `paseo-closure.test.ts` also checks that `agent.ts` is the only closure file of kind `adapted` for a logic reason, apart from the removals above and, once V7 lands, V7's two recorded removals, that its on-disk SHA-256 equals the integrated hash in `closure.json`, that its pinned source hash is unchanged, and that applying the recorded exception to the pinned source reproduces the integrated file.
- [ ] `claude-sdk-boundary.test.ts` feeds raw `SDKMessage` sequences through an injected `queryFactory` into `createClaudeBackend`, then `startThread` and `runStreamed`, and into the run-card reducer. It covers a duplicate `init`, streamed text and thinking, a compact boundary, cache tokens, the model context window, array tool results, an `assistant` API error followed by an empty `success`, and the Bash description. Each expected card or event equals V3's for the same input. It also covers the history repair against a fixture session file. The old API error matrix runs unchanged through the same boundary.
- [ ] The ported Paseo tests under `packages/daemon/test/paseo/` pass. Run `npm test -w packages/daemon -- paseo-closure claude-permission claude-sdk-boundary paseo/`.

**Verify, live.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A PR is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked. Ten lanes on the configured `swarm workers` model at the PR head, per the boot recipe.

- [ ] Lane 1. Regression lane against trunk. Ask the same question in a Claude group at V3 head and at V6 head. Save `turn.txt`. Pass when both run cards finish and the V6 card shows the same sections.
- [ ] Lane 2. Send a follow-up while a long turn runs. Save `steer.txt`. Pass when the follow-up joins the same turn instead of queueing.
- [ ] Lane 3. Click stop during a `sleep 30` tool call. Save `stop.txt`. Pass when the card shows stopped within 2 s and the next message resumes the session.
- [ ] Lane 4. In `qa` mode ask Claude to write a file. Save `qa-write.txt`. Pass when the write is refused and no file appears.
- [ ] Lane 5. In `write` mode ask Claude to edit a file in the project. Save `write.txt`. Pass when the file changes.
- [ ] Lane 6. Restart the daemon and reply in the same topic. Save `resume.txt`. Pass when Claude answers with context from the earlier turn.
- [ ] Lane 7. Open the model list in the group settings card. Save `models.txt`. Pass when the list comes from the provider catalog without a throwaway turn.
- [ ] Lane 8. Run `/compact` in a long session. Save `compact.txt`. Pass when the card reports compaction.
- [ ] Lane 9. Remove the `claude` binary from `PATH` and send a message. Save `missing.txt`. Pass when the card shows a clear not-installed error.
- [ ] Lane 10. Check that a user hook in `~/.claude/settings.json` does not loop back into the bot. Save `hooks.txt`. Pass when one turn produces one card.

**Verify, perf.** Tests alone are not sufficient verification. A PR is verified only when its unit, live, and perf boxes are all checked.

- [ ] Metric. Time from message receipt to the first streamed token on the card, and time from stop click to stopped.
- [ ] Probe. Interleave five turns on the bridge Claude backend at V3 head and five on the new provider at V6 head, timed from the daemon log.
- [ ] Baseline. Record the V3 numbers first.
- [ ] Rule. Fail if first-token latency is more than 10% slower or stop takes longer than 2 s.

**Review evidence.** Keep the prescribed captures and independent verdict before guarded Shipping.

## Add ACP, Copilot, OpenCode, and Pi providers (V7)

**Depends on.** V6. All provider code starts from V6's branch at `F6`, not at V6 code-ready. The three mechanical leaves in the dependency table may be developed first, and they satisfy no provider acceptance on their own. The provider IDs and the picker wait until V4 has merged, and the root writes the provider IDs in `domain.ts`. Lane 7 needs V4's web UI. V7 merges after V6.

**Files.**

- [ ] Copy the files *Import closure* lists to the destinations V6's *Import closure* defines, so `acp-agent.ts`, `generic-acp-agent.ts`, and `copilot-acp-agent.ts` sit flat in `packages/daemon/src/agent/providers/` beside `opencode/` and `pi/`, and extend `agent/paseo/closure.json` with V7's closure. Create the Vonvon `paseo-home.ts` at the destination of `server/paseo-home.ts` and the OpenCode version-gate wrapper.
- [ ] Edit `packages/daemon/src/agent/index.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/domain.ts`, and the provider picker in `packages/ui/src/`.
- [ ] Create the four wrappers, `packages/daemon/src/agent/custom-acp-command.ts`, and the tests under *Wrapper contracts*. Edit the files that section names: `agent/catalog.ts`, `agent/backend-loader.ts`, `agent/paseo/session-backend.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/{domain,collaboration,settings}.ts`, `admin/{collaboration,groups,host-settings}.ts`, `server/rpc/{collaboration,command}.ts`, `server/snapshot.ts`, `host/bootstrap.ts`, `bot/supervisor.ts`, `packages/ui/src/{App,JoinGroupDialog}.tsx`, and `packages/ui/src/settings/SettingsPanel.tsx`.
- [ ] Create `packages/daemon/test/paseo-closure-v7.test.ts`, or extend `paseo-closure.test.ts`, for the checks below.
- [ ] Edit `NOTICE`.

*Import closure (issue 74).* The pinned inventory reaches 129 sources and assets from eight roots at Paseo `0009a7e71`. It is an inventory, not a copy list. V7 copies exactly the files named below and follows V6's *Import closure* rules for them: whole modules, specifier changes only apart from the recorded edits below, each file in `closure.json` with its pinned SHA-256, every provider `Logger` type import through V6's `logger.ts`, including the `jsonl-rpc-process.ts` leaf, and every other unresolved import a blocker. Paths are relative to Paseo `packages/server/src/server/agent/providers/` unless prefixed otherwise. `agent/` paths are relative to `packages/server/src/server/`; `server/` and `utils/` paths are relative to `packages/server/src/`; `packages/` paths are repository-relative.

- *Compiler contract.* The daemon type-checks `src/**` and `test/**` with `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `noImplicitOverride`, `noFallthroughCasesInSwitch`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, and `lib` ES2022, and `npm run typecheck` is a required gate. The pinned Paseo files were written for Paseo's own settings, `strict` with `lib` ES2023 and none of the other three. The ported tests are also not type-clean against the pinned sources and SDK types, apart from any daemon flag. For example, three test subclasses override `ACPAgentSession`'s private `spawnProcess`, and the OpenCode fixtures use event and return shapes that `@opencode/client` `2.0.10` does not declare. At the copy checkpoint, 513 selected tests passed and the daemon's `tsc --noEmit` reported 128 diagnostics, 14 in production and 114 in tests. V7 makes these files type-check with changes that leave emitted JavaScript identical, and keeps every strict option.
  - Compiler options. `packages/daemon/tsconfig.json` changes only `lib`, from `["ES2022"]` to `["ES2022", "ES2023.Array", "ES2024.Promise"]`. `ES2023.Array` declares the `findLast` and `findLastIndex` that `opencode/v2/structured-output.ts:14` and `pi/agent.ts:853,861` call. `ES2024.Promise` declares the `Promise.withResolvers` that only `pi/agent.test.ts` calls. Node 22.12, the daemon's `engines` floor, has both. No file under `packages/daemon/src/` may call `Promise.withResolvers`, so production relies on no ES2024 runtime API. `target`, `module`, `types`, `include`, `exclude`, and every strict and module-syntax option stay unchanged. These two changes resolve 10 diagnostics with no byte edit, and the logger retarget above resolves the one at `pi/cli-runtime.ts:76`.
  - Allowed edits. Each edit inserts or replaces syntax that TypeScript and esbuild erase, in one of five forms, where E1 has one named sub-form.
    - E1, non-null assertion. A postfix `!` on an expression that the diagnostic reports as possibly `undefined` or `null`: TS2532, TS2531, TS18048, or TS2322 and TS2345 where the only mismatch is `| undefined` or `| null`. Under Paseo's settings an indexed access already has the non-undefined type, so E1 restores the type upstream compiled with. Example: `const arg = args[index]!;` at `generic-acp-agent.ts:195`.
      - E1s, shorthand expansion. When the expression E1 would mark is a shorthand property `name` of an object literal, the shorthand has no position for `!`. The edit replaces the identifier `name` with `name: name!`. The replaced text is exactly the identifier, and the inserted text is exactly `name: name!` for the same identifier. TypeScript and esbuild print the explicit same-name property as the shorthand, so the emitted JavaScript is unchanged, and the identity proof checks it for the file. At the checkpoint that is only `pi/cli-runtime.ts:63`, where `const [command, ...args] = launch.argv;` gives `command` the type `string | undefined` under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, and the object literal at `:62-67` needs `string`. The ledger then holds 102 edits in 18 files, and every residual diagnostic is owned. Codex reruns the identity proof on the applied edit and records `esbuild.version`; the expected pre and post JavaScript SHA-256 is the one `v7-identity-proof.json` already records for this replacement, and any other value is a blocker returned to Claude Code.
    - E2, `override` on a member that TS4114 flags. At the checkpoint that is only `opencode/http-error.ts:2`.
    - E3, `type` on an import specifier or declaration that TS1484 flags. It removes only an import that the file uses only as a type.
    - E4, in ported tests and test-only fixtures only. A type assertion `as T`, or `as unknown as T` where TS2352 refuses `as T`, an explicit type argument on a generic call, or a type annotation on a fixture binding. `T` is a type expression over types in scope from the pinned sources, including a type the pinned file itself declares, the pinned SDKs, Node, or vitest. It may use only these constructs. A type reference with optional type arguments, including TypeScript's standard utility types. `typeof` of a binding in scope. An indexed access with a string or number literal, or with `number`. An array, tuple, union, intersection or parenthesized type of such types, with `readonly` or `keyof`. The keyword types `void`, `unknown`, `undefined`, `null`, `never`, `string`, `number` and `boolean`, and string or number literal types. A function type whose parameter types and return type are such types, with a rest parameter allowed. An object type literal only as a type argument of `Extract` or `Exclude`, each member a property signature whose type is a literal or keyword type. It never contains `any`, a mapped, conditional, template literal, constructor or `import()` type, a type predicate, or an object type literal anywhere else. At the checkpoint the two ACP sites `acp-agent.test.ts:1513` and `:3314` therefore use ` as ACPSessionInternals["connection"]`, the indexed access over the interface the pinned test declares at `:98-105`, in place of an authored object type, and the OpenCode harness annotation at `v2-harness.ts:66`, `: (...args: Parameters<V2Api["session"]["prompt"]>) => Promise<void>`, stays, because `:152` calls `this.prompt(input, options)` with two arguments and no reference-only spelling exists. Codex reruns the identity proof for `acp-agent.test.ts` after the two replacements. E4 never wraps the argument of `expect` or a matcher's expected value. A fixture whose shape differs from `@opencode/client` `2.0.10` or `@agentclientprotocol/sdk` keeps its runtime shape. No fixture gains an SDK field, such as `durable`, or loses one, such as `created`, and the PR lists each such site with the SDK field that differs.
    - E5. `private` becomes `protected` on `ACPAgentSession.spawnProcess` in `acp-agent.ts`, the method that the ported subclasses at `acp-agent.test.ts:3527`, `:3563`, and `:3908` override. Accessibility modifiers emit nothing. No other member changes.
    - Never allowed: `any`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, `@ts-nocheck`, `eslint-disable`, a new type or interface declaration, any compiler option other than `lib`, a tsconfig `exclude` or per-file override, a dropped, skipped, or `.only` test case beyond the recorded drops, or any change to a value, field, call, branch, `expect`, or matcher.
  - Scope. E1 to E3 may apply to copied V7 production files, ported tests, and test-only fixtures. `registry.ts` and `bridge.ts` take none, because they have no diagnostic. A residual diagnostic that none of E1, E1s, E2 to E5 resolves is a blocker returned to Claude Code. It is never fixed another way.
  - Ledger. After the `lib` change and the logger retarget, and before any edit, the root runs the daemon typecheck and saves the output as the residual log. Every edit resolves at least one diagnostic in that log, on the same statement or on the declaration whose type the diagnostic reports, and every diagnostic in the log is resolved by a recorded edit. In `closure.json`, an affected file has the kind `type-only`, or keeps `ported-test-case-exclusions` when it also has dropped cases. Its entry records `preTypeEditSha256`, the integrated SHA-256 after the edits and the notice, `typeOnlyEdits`, each with its `start`, `end`, line, column, form, `replacedText`, `insertedText`, and the codes and positions of the diagnostics it resolves, and the `modificationNotice` that *Modification notice* below defines. Offsets. Every `start` and `end` is an offset in the pre-edit text, whose SHA-256 is `preTypeEditSha256`, in the unit the existing V7 ledger and its `apply.mjs` already use. `end` minus `start` is the length of `replacedText`, which is empty for an insertion. The edits of one file and its notice are listed in strictly ascending `start`, and no `end` exceeds the next `start`. Forward replay works on the pre-edit bytes in descending `start` order, so no edit shifts another. Before each edit it asserts that the pre-edit slice from `start` to `end` equals `replacedText`, then replaces that slice with `insertedText`. Reverse replay works on the integrated bytes in ascending `start` order. Every earlier edit is already reverted, so the edit's integrated slice begins at its pre-edit `start` and has the length of `insertedText`. The tool asserts that this slice equals `insertedText`, then replaces it with `replacedText`, which restores pre-edit coordinates for every later edit. A tool may instead translate each `start` to its integrated offset, `start` plus the sum over every earlier edit of the `insertedText` length minus the `replacedText` length, and revert in descending integrated order with the same assertion and the same replacement by `replacedText`. Both orders rebuild the same original slice for every edit. A slice that differs from `insertedText`, a wrong order, or a stale offset fails by naming the edit instead of producing a wrong hash. Forward replay must reproduce `integratedSha256` and reverse replay `preTypeEditSha256`. The E1s, E4, and E5 edits are replacements, and V11's two `!` are the insertion case with empty `replacedText`. Replay does not replace the sealed diagnostic ownership check under *Gates*.
  - Modification notice. `packages/daemon/LICENSE.paseo` section 4(b) requires modified files to carry prominent notices stating that the files were changed, so a file with type-only edits carries an in-file notice as well as the `NOTICE` entry, and the ledger records and proves that notice like an edit. It is the file's `modificationNotice`, with form `N`, `replacedText` empty, and a pre-edit `start` before every type-only edit of the file. That `start` is 0, or, when an earlier stage already wrote a notice at the top, such as the line-1 case-drop notice of `acp-agent.test.ts`, the end of that notice, which stays. If the file starts with `#!`, `start` is after that line. `insertedText` is one to three whole lines, each `// ` followed by printable ASCII and a line feed. The file's first notice line starts with `// Modified from Paseo`, and the inserted lines state that the file has type-only edits erased at compile time and give the repository path of the `closure.json` that records them. No inserted line contains `@`, `/*`, `*/`, `eslint`, or `<reference`, so the notice is not a legal comment that esbuild keeps, and not a TypeScript, JSX, or lint directive. `N` owns no diagnostic and does not count as a type-only edit, but forward and reverse replay include it under *Ledger*. The closure test scans `insertedText` with `ts.createScanner`, keeping trivia, and accepts only `SingleLineCommentTrivia`, `NewLineTrivia`, and `WhitespaceTrivia`. It also checks that each file with type-only edits has exactly one `N`. No upstream comment or other byte is deleted or rewritten for the notice.
  - Identity proof. For every file with type-only edits, `esbuild.transform` from the repository's esbuild, with `loader: "ts"`, `format: "esm"`, `target: "es2022"`, no minification, no source map, and `tsconfigRaw` `{"compilerOptions": {"verbatimModuleSyntax": false, "useDefineForClassFields": true}}`, gives byte-identical output for the pre-edit and the post-edit file. The post-edit file includes the modification notice, which esbuild drops as a non-legal comment, so the same comparison covers it. esbuild is the transformer of the daemon build, and it prints from the syntax tree, so an erased type or a grouping parenthesis is not a difference.
  - Gates, on the integrated V7 head.
    - `npm run typecheck` at the root and in `packages/daemon` exits 0 with no diagnostics, and `tsc --showConfig -p packages/daemon` has the same `compilerOptions` as `main` except `lib`, and the source tsconfig keeps identical declared `include` and `exclude`. The expanded `files` list is not compared, because V7 adds files.
    - The residual log, the ledger, and the edits match one to one, and the identity proof holds for every edited file. The tool for this gate is `scripts/verify-type-ledger.mjs`, Vonvon-owned at the repository root, which Codex writes in V7. It replaces the checkpoint's `.audit/v7-type-implementation/verify-ledger.py`, which predates E1s. That script routes every form other than E1, E2, E3, and E5 to an assertion that the form is E4 in a test path, and it requires exactly one residual diagnostic at `pi/cli-runtime.ts:63`, so it rejects a correct E1s edit and is not rerun as a gate. The new tool reads the sealed residual log, whose SHA-256 the PR records, and the ledger. It requires that every diagnostic in the log is owned by exactly one edit and that every edit owns at least one, which at the checkpoint is 117 owned diagnostics across 102 edits; that every form is one of E1, E1s, E2, E3, E4, or E5 under the form checks below, with E1s accepted in `src/**`; that forward and reverse replay reproduce the hashes per *Ledger*; and that a fresh `tsc --noEmit -p packages/daemon` reports zero diagnostics. It writes `ledger-verification.json` with every integrated hash and the counts, saved with the PR beside the identity proof. The residual log stays the one the root saved before any edit, never a rerun, so the sealed baseline ownership check keeps its meaning.
    - The closure test checks each recorded edit's form. The inserted or replaced text is `!`, `override `, `type `, `protected` in place of `private`, for E1s an identifier replaced by `name: name!` for the same identifier at a shorthand position, with the non-whitespace character before it `{` or `,` and after it `,` or `}`, or for E4 an ` as ` clause, a type-argument list, or a type annotation. For E4 the test wraps the edit text into a parseable statement, parses it with `ts.createSourceFile` from the daemon's `typescript` devDependency, and accepts only the node kinds the E4 grammar lists, failing on any other node kind and naming the edit. The test carries negative cases for an authored object type, `any`, a mapped type, and an E4 text in a `src/**` path, so it is shown to detect the defects it exists for. Type and directive rule. The test parses each edited file's pre-edit and integrated text with `ts.createSourceFile` and counts `SyntaxKind.AnyKeyword` nodes, which are exactly the `any` type. The integrated count equals the pre-edit count, and no parsed edit text contains one. It collects every comment of both texts from the parsed tree, with `ts.getLeadingCommentRanges` at each node's full start and `ts.getTrailingCommentRanges` at its end over every node and token from `getChildren`, deduplicated by position. A raw `ts.createScanner` pass over a whole file is not used, because it loses sync at template literals and regular expressions and misses comments. A comment that contains `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, `@ts-nocheck`, `eslint-disable`, or `eslint-enable` is a suppression directive. The integrated count of directives equals the pre-edit count, and no `insertedText` contains one. The word any in a comment, a string, or an identifier is not an `any` type and passes, because the rule reads syntax kinds, not substrings. Negative cases add ` as any` through an edit, `: any` outside any edit, and a `// @ts-expect-error` line, each to an in-memory copy, and each fails this rule on its own, not only through a hash. E4 appears only in ported tests and fixtures, and E5 only on `ACPAgentSession.spawnProcess`. No `src/**` file imports `pino` or calls `Promise.withResolvers`.
    - At least the 39 test files and 513 tests from the checkpoint pass under `npm test -w packages/daemon -- paseo/providers`, with the same 16 recorded case drops and no new skip or `.only`. The full daemon suite, build, and lint pass.
    - Two independent audit lanes read every edit against the residual log and confirm that no assertion, value, call, or fixture field changed.

*Wrapper contracts.* The four wrappers are Vonvon code. Each builds a `SessionBackend` from V6's `session-backend.ts` around one copied client and changes no copied byte. Facts below cite the V7 head `e9203c6`; paths are relative to `packages/daemon/src/agent/`.

- Identifiers. Each new provider has one `Tool` value, one backend id, and the copied client's Paseo provider name.

  | `Tool` (`toolSchema`) | Backend id | Copied client | Paseo `provider` | Display name |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `codex` | `codex-appserver` | unchanged | unchanged | Codex |
  | `claude` | `claude-agent` | unchanged | `claude` | Claude |
  | `copilot` | `copilot-acp` | `CopilotACPAgentClient` | `copilot` | Copilot |
  | `opencode` | `opencode-server` | `OpenCodeV2AgentClient` | `opencode` | OpenCode |
  | `pi` | `pi-rpc` | `PiRpcAgentClient` | `pi` | Pi |
  | `acp` | `custom-acp` | `GenericACPAgentClient` | `acp` | 自定义 ACP |

  - `packages/protocol/src/domain.ts` is the one source. `toolSchema` becomes `z.enum(["codex", "claude", "copilot", "opencode", "pi", "acp"])`. A new `backendIdSchema` is `z.enum(["codex-appserver", "claude-agent", "copilot-acp", "opencode-server", "pi-rpc", "custom-acp"])`, and `backendByTool` is `{ codex: "codex-appserver", claude: "claude-agent", copilot: "copilot-acp", opencode: "opencode-server", pi: "pi-rpc", acp: "custom-acp" } as const satisfies Record<Tool, BackendId>`. There is no separate `ProviderId`. The plan's earlier "provider id" means `Tool`.
  - Every tool-to-backend conversion uses `backendByTool`: `server/rpc/collaboration.ts:65` and `server/rpc/command.ts:43`, which today map anything other than `codex` to `claude-agent`. The local `backend` enum in `protocol/src/collaboration.ts:12`, the `'codex-appserver' | 'claude-agent'` unions in `admin/collaboration.ts:9` and `admin/groups.ts:15`, and the literal checks at `admin/collaboration.ts:49` and `admin/groups.ts:34` become `BackendId` and `backendIdSchema`.
  - Fields that already carry a free `string` backend stay `string`: `membershipSchema.backend`, `projectRecordSchema.backend`, the bound-session and `history` `backend`, the `resume` action's `backend`, and the session settings `identity.backend`. Stored projects keep their values, and `backendForProject` still falls back for unknown ids.
  - `desktopSnapshot.tools` gains one `ToolState` for each new `Tool`, built in `server/snapshot.ts` from `createBackend(id).doctor({ force: true })` with Claude's existing mapping. `environmentToolSchema` (`codex`, `claude`, `lark-cli`) is the managed-install list and does not change.
  - `catalog.ts` gains four entries with the ids, display names, and `supportedModes: ['full']`. `access` is `'acp'` for `copilot-acp` and `custom-acp`; `BackendAccess` gains `'server'` for `opencode-server` and `'rpc'` for `pi-rpc`. `dep` is `external-cli` with `binName` `copilot`, `opencode`, and `pi`, the copied default binaries (`copilot-acp-agent.ts:87`, `opencode/v2/runtime.ts:153`, `pi/agent.ts:98`). `custom-acp` uses the new `DepKind` `'host-command'` described below. `index.ts` `REGISTRY` gains the four factories, and the catalog and registry id sets stay equal.
  - UI. `JoinGroupDialog.tsx:91` builds its options from `snapshot.tools`: `codex` always, and any other `Tool` whose `installation` is `installed`. `App.tsx:68` `backendName` covers the six backend ids. Lane 7 checks the first rule.
- Permission modes. Every new backend has `supportedModes: ['full']`. V3 grants `qa` and `write` only where an operating-system sandbox enforces them. Codex has its sandbox, and Claude runs those modes with `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true` (`session-backend.ts` `claudeSessionConfigFor`). None of the copied clients has such a sandbox. OpenCode's permission rules (`opencode/options.ts`) are policy inside the agent process, and `bash` reaches any path. The existing filters then apply unchanged: `projectCreatableBackends`, `backendOptionsFor` (`bot/handle-message.ts:383`), `assertBackendUsable` and `bindModeFor` (`project/lifecycle.ts:22-38`), and the tier guards in `admin/ops.ts:148,255`. A group with a `qa` or `write` tier cannot select these backends, and its tiers cannot be lowered while it uses one.
  - `full` config. `copilot-acp` and `custom-acp` use `{ provider, cwd, featureValues: { auto_accept: true } }`. This is the copied unattended rule (`acp-agent.ts:880-890`). `requestPermission` then selects the allow option for every request except a chooser (`acp-agent.ts:2557-2571`). `opencode-server` uses `{ provider: 'opencode', cwd, featureValues: { auto_accept: true } }`, which `opencode/v2/permissions.ts:95` honors with no `toolPolicy`. `pi-rpc` uses `{ provider: 'pi', cwd }`, because Pi has no permission gate and `setMode` throws (`pi/agent.ts:1443`). No wrapper sets `modeId`. OpenCode then uses its `build` agent (`v2/agent.ts` `createSession`), Copilot uses its session default, and a custom ACP agent uses its own default. `network` is not consulted, because `full` includes network.
  - Permission reply. `capabilities.approvals` stays `false`, as V6's constructor requires. A request that still reaches `SessionBackendThread`, such as an ACP chooser or a Pi extension UI request, gets the existing deny through `session.respondToPermission`. Nothing changes on the wire. The fixture tests show the permission reply for each. For OpenCode it is the copied allow under `auto_accept` (`opencode/v2/permissions.ts:95-96`), with no `permission_requested` reaching Vonvon. For Pi it is the deny of an extension UI request through `respondToPermission`.
- Capabilities, from the copied session APIs.

  | Backend | `goal` | `steer` | `compact` | `resume` | `approvals` |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `copilot-acp` | true | false | false | true | false |
  | `opencode-server` | true | true | true | true | false |
  | `pi-rpc` | true | true | true | true | false |
  | `custom-acp` | true | false | false | true | false |

  - `goal`. `SessionBackendThread.runGoal` is a prompt over `startTurn` and needs nothing from the provider.
  - `steer`. `SessionBackendThread.supportsSteer` checks for `steerActiveTurn`. `OpenCodeV2Session` has it (`opencode/v2/session.ts:387`), and so does the Pi session (`pi/agent.ts:1347`). `ACPAgentSession` does not. The error text in `SessionBackendThread.steer` becomes `当前回合无法追加消息`, without the word Claude.
  - `compact`. OpenCode turns `/compact` into `session.compact` (`opencode/v2/turns.ts:251-253`), and its timeline emits a `compaction` item (`v2/timeline.ts:60,189`). The existing `runStreamed('/compact')` path works. Pi handles `/compact` only through `tryHandleOutOfBand` (`pi/agent.ts:1608-1624`). `startTurn` sends the text to Pi as a prompt. `SessionBackendThread.compact()` therefore first calls `this.session.tryHandleOutOfBand?.('/compact')`. If a handler returns, the method runs it with an `emit` callback and returns `{ compacted, usage: null }`. `compacted` is true when a `timeline` event with a `compaction` item of status `completed` reaches `emit` or the session subscription while the handler runs. A `timeline` `assistant_message` whose text starts with `[Error] ` throws that text, matching `executeCompactCommand` (`pi/agent.ts:1778-1815`). Otherwise the existing path runs. Claude and Codex define no `tryHandleOutOfBand`, so their path does not change. No ACP code emits a `compaction` item, so ACP and Copilot report `compact: false`.
  - `resume`. Each copied client has `listImportableSessions` and `resumeSession`, and each session has `streamHistory`. ACP lists only when the agent advertises `sessionCapabilities.list` and returns `[]` otherwise (`acp-agent.ts:1218`). ACP resume uses `loadSession`, then `unstable_resumeSession`, and otherwise throws the copied `does not support ACP session resume` error (`acp-agent.ts:1844-1866`). Vonvon passes that limit through and does not hide the capability.
- History through `SessionBackend`. V6's constructor requires `listThreads` and `readHistory` whenever `resume` is true. `session-backend.ts` gains one exported helper that every wrapper passes as both readers:
  - `listThreads(cwd, limit)` maps `client.listImportableSessions({ cwd, limit })` exactly as the existing fallback in `SessionBackend.listThreads` does.
  - `readHistory(cwd, sessionId, maxTurns)` does not call `importSession`. `importSessionFromPersistence` resumes a live session and then awaits `collectImportedHistory(session.streamHistory())` with no `catch` or `finally` (`paseo/provider-session-import.ts:35-36`). Pi's `streamHistory` awaits `requestEntryCapture` and `runtimeSession.getMessages()` (`pi/agent.ts:1411-1414`), and OpenCode's awaits `messages(...)` (`opencode/v2/session.ts:261-264`). If either rejects, the caller never receives the session and cannot close it. The helper therefore owns the session directly.
    - It builds the handle that `buildImportPersistenceHandle` builds: `{ provider: client.provider, sessionId, nativeHandle: sessionId, metadata: { ...config, provider: client.provider, cwd } }`. `config` is the wrapper's `configFor({ cwd, mode: 'full' })`.
    - It calls `const session = await client.resumeSession(handle, config)`. If `resumeSession` rejects, no session was returned. The copied client releases what it started: ACP through `closeAfterInitializationFailure` (`acp-agent.ts:1868-1870`), OpenCode through `session.close()` in `attach` (`opencode/v2/agent.ts` `attach`), and Pi through `runtimeSession.close()` and the file cleanups (`pi/agent.ts:2611-2614`).
    - After `resumeSession` resolves, the helper owns the session. In `try`, it iterates `session.streamHistory()` and keeps each `timeline` event's `item` and `timestamp`, the same filter as `collectImportedHistory`. In `finally`, it calls `await session.close().catch(() => undefined)`. A close failure never hides the history error, and the session is closed exactly once on both success and failure.
    - It passes no `model`, `modeId`, or thinking override. The read runs no turn, and without those overrides OpenCode's `applyResumeOverrides` switches nothing. For Pi, the Pi proxy's `resumeSession` already sets `nativeHandle`, and the handle above sets the same value.
    - Timeline items fold into `HistoryTurn`s. A `user_message` opens a turn and sets `userText`. `assistant_message` text and `reasoning` text append to the open turn. A `tool_call` adds or updates one `HistoryTool` per `callId`, using `toolTitle` and `toolOutput`, with `exitCode` as in `mapTimeline` and `failed` when the status is `failed`. `startedAt` comes from the entry `timestamp` in seconds. The result keeps the last `maxTurns` turns, and `totalTurns` counts the non-empty turns. `SessionBackend.readHistory` already turns any failure into an empty history.
  - The `AgentBackend.readHistory` contract (`types.ts:424-429`) means no turn starts, and the session is not held. The helper resumes without a turn and always closes. The history card therefore pays one provider process start, measured in the perf section only if the card is part of a probe.
  - Failure fixtures. A test with a fake `AgentClient` resolves `resumeSession` with a session whose `streamHistory` yields one `timeline` item and then rejects. It checks that `close` ran exactly once and that `readHistory` returned the empty history. A second case has `close` reject too and checks the same result. With the copied fixtures, `fake-pi.ts` fails `getMessages` after start, and `v2-harness.ts` fails `message.list`. Each checks that the fixture's process or connection was released. If a copied fixture cannot inject the failure without a change beyond *Compiler contract* E4, the fake-client test stands for that provider, and the PR names the fixture.
- Pi session key. `PiRpcAgentClient.resumeSession` needs `handle.nativeHandle`, the session file (`pi/agent.ts:2550-2553`). `PiRpcAgentSession.id` is the session UUID (`pi/agent.ts:1259`). `describePersistence()` returns that UUID with `nativeHandle: state.sessionFile` (`pi/agent.ts:1473-1484`). `listPiImportableSessions` returns `providerHandleId` as the file path, and nothing public maps a UUID to a file (`pi/session-descriptor.ts:73-101,218-230`). A UUID stored by Vonvon therefore cannot be resumed after a restart without new state.
  - Decision. For `pi-rpc`, Vonvon's session id is the absolute session file path. This is the one value that create, list, resume, and history share. No mapping is persisted, and the stored and wire shape stays a string, within the `sessionId` limit of 1000 in `protocol/src/collaboration.ts:6`.
  - Adapter. The Pi wrapper wraps the copied client in a `Proxy`, as `claude-provider.ts` `bridgeClient` does, and changes no copied byte. `SessionBackend` does not change for this.
    - `createSession` and `resumeSession` return the session wrapped in a second `Proxy`. Its `id` reads `describePersistence()?.nativeHandle`. If that value is missing, the adapter closes the session and throws `Pi 未返回会话文件，无法恢复`. `rpc-types.ts:96` declares `sessionFile` optional, and only `internal` sessions omit it. Every other property reads from the target with the target as receiver, and functions are bound to the target. `SessionBackendThread.sessionId` then holds the file path, and `supportsSteer` still sees `steerActiveTurn`.
    - `resumeSession(handle, ...)` passes `{ ...handle, nativeHandle: handle.nativeHandle ?? handle.sessionId }` to the copied client. `SessionBackend.resumeThread` builds `{ provider, sessionId: options.sessionId, metadata: { cwd } }`, so after a restart `nativeHandle` is the stored file path. `buildResumeConfig` takes `cwd` from the metadata (`pi/agent.ts:467`).
    - `listThreads` keeps the helper unchanged. `providerHandleId` is already the file path.
    - `readHistory` keeps the helper unchanged. The helper's handle sets `nativeHandle` to the file path, which is the value `buildImportPersistenceHandle` would set for a listed session.
    - Boundary check. Before `resumeSession` and `readHistory`, the adapter requires the id to be an absolute path that ends in `.jsonl`. Anything else is rejected with `无效的 Pi 会话` and never reaches Pi. The `resume` RPC takes `sessionId` from a client, so the check sits at this entry.
    - Pi's own events still carry the UUID, for example `thread_started` (`pi/agent.ts:2143`). `mapEvent` turns that into `{ type: 'system', threadId }`, and no bot or card code reads it.
  - Tests. A fixture-driven test with `fake-pi.ts` creates a session, checks that `thread.sessionId` equals the fixture's session file, builds a new backend as a restart would, calls `resumeThread({ sessionId: <file>, cwd })`, and checks that the copied client received `nativeHandle` equal to that file. It also lists and reads history for the same file, and shows a UUID or relative path rejected before Pi is called.
- Custom ACP command. Lane 5 configures it in host settings.
  - Protocol. `hostSectionSchemas.execution` gains `customAcpCommand: z.array(z.string().min(1).max(4096)).min(1).max(64).nullable()`. It is an argv list, because `GenericACPAgentClient` takes `command: [string, ...string[]]` and runs it with no shell (`generic-acp-agent.ts:20-23,68-73`). The host `execution` edit patch then accepts it with no other change to `editSchema`. `null` means not configured.
  - Storage. `<appDir>/custom-acp.json` holds `{ "version": 1, "command": string[] | null }`. A new `agent/custom-acp-command.ts` reads and saves it the way `service/codex-bin.ts` handles `service-environment.json`: synchronous atomic replace, mode `0600`, ENOENT reads as `null`, and a corrupt or invalid record throws with the file path and one fixed reason. `readCustomAcpCommand(appDir?): [string, ...string[]] | null` and `saveCustomAcpCommand(value: [string, ...string[]] | null, appDir?): void` are both synchronous. It is a separate file because `saveServiceCodexBin` rewrites the whole `service-environment.json` record.
  - State. The module exports one captured state:

    ```ts
    export type CustomAcpInvalidReason = 'unreadable' | 'not-json' | 'unsupported-version' | 'invalid-command';
    export type CustomAcpCommandState =
      | { kind: 'unset' }
      | { kind: 'set'; command: [string, ...string[]] }
      | { kind: 'invalid'; file: string; reason: CustomAcpInvalidReason };
    ```

    `invalid` carries no error text. Node 22's `JSON.parse` error quotes part of the input, and a validation message could quote an argument. Each reason maps to one fixed Chinese sentence in the module, and `file` is always `<appDir>/custom-acp.json`. The read throw follows the same rule. `customAcpStateSchema` is the zod schema of this type and reuses the command validation. `resolveCustomAcpCommand(appDir): CustomAcpCommandState` never throws. It maps `null` to `unset`, a command to `set`, and each read throw to `invalid` with its reason.
  - Capture. The daemon reads the file once, in the top-level host, and every process uses that value until the host restarts. `daemon run` has more than one process. The supervisor hosts `startApi` and the snapshot (`supervisor.ts:168`), and each bot's orchestrator runs in a child spawned as `daemon run --bot <appId>` that the supervisor respawns after a crash or an activation toggle without restarting itself (`supervisor.ts:85-135`). A child that read the file itself would run a newly saved command while the supervisor still reported the old one.
    - `initCustomAcpCommand(): Promise<CustomAcpCommandState>` is the one capture. `runHostRuntime` in `host/bootstrap.ts` awaits it right after `if (control.requested) return;`, before the `codexBin` block and `runRun`. `runRun`'s only caller is `runHostRuntime` (`bootstrap.ts:27`), so the value is captured before `startBridge` creates the orchestrator (`bridge.ts:92`) and before `startApi` builds the snapshot.
    - Without `process.send` the process is the top-level host, as `bootstrap.ts:14` already assumes. Only `supervisor.ts:92` spawns with `'ipc'` stdio. It stores `resolveCustomAcpCommand(paths.appDir)`. A single-bot run from a shell or from `host/client.ts` is its own host and takes this branch.
    - With `process.send` the process is a supervised child. It adds a `'message'` listener, sends `{ fcb: 'fcb.custom-acp.req' }`, and waits up to 10 s for `{ fcb: 'fcb.custom-acp', state }` whose `state` passes `customAcpStateSchema`. It then removes the listener and stores the state. On a timeout or a schema failure it throws `未收到主进程的自定义 ACP 设置` and never reads the file, so the child exits and the supervisor's existing backoff restarts it. The child asks first because Node emits `'message'` only to listeners that already exist, and a push right after spawn can arrive before the child has loaded. The existing admin IPC cannot carry the value, because the child registers its responder only after `startBridge` returns (`run.ts:73-93`).
    - The supervisor's existing child `'message'` handler (`supervisor.ts:107-112`) gains one branch. When `c.proc === proc` and the message parses as `fcb.custom-acp.req`, it sends `{ fcb: 'fcb.custom-acp', state: customAcpCommand() }` if `proc.connected`. Every respawn gets the same captured value. Both messages carry the `fcb` tag and are parsed with zod like `ownedWorkSchema`. The command does not go through argv or the environment, because a 64 by 4096 command can exceed Linux's 128 KB `MAX_ARG_STRLEN` for one environment string and fail the spawn. The IPC channel's `'json'` serialization has no such limit.
    - `customAcpCommand(): CustomAcpCommandState` returns the captured state, and every consumer below reads only it. With nothing captured and no `process.send`, as in a CLI command that never starts the daemon, it captures through `resolveCustomAcpCommand`. With nothing captured in a supervised child it throws, because that means `initCustomAcpCommand` did not run.
    - The top-level host logs `custom-acp-init` with the state's `kind`, plus `file` and `reason` for `invalid`. No process logs the command, the IPC payload, or a raw read or parse error.
  - Validation, in that module for both read and save. 1 to 64 elements. Each element is 1 to 4096 characters with no NUL, CR, or LF. The first element is either an absolute path or a bare command name with no path separator, because the daemon's working directory is not the user's. Arguments are stored verbatim and the command can hold anything the user types. The view shows them back to the owner only. They are never logged, and diagnostics show them only through the copied `getDiagnostic` on request.
  - Host settings. `admin/host-settings.ts` adds `customAcpCommand` to `stored` from a fresh `resolveCustomAcpCommand` on each view, and to `effective` from `customAcpCommand()`: the command for `set`, `null` otherwise. It is added to the `restart` `ApplyEffect` fields. The orchestrator caches one backend per id for the daemon's lifetime (`bot/handle-message.ts:667-677`), so a change applies after restart, like `codexBin`. `save` validates and writes the file with `saveCustomAcpCommand`.
    - Revision. The section `revision` hash keeps its `codexBin` part and adds `customAcpCommand`: the stored command, `null`, or `{ invalid: <SHA-256 of the file bytes> }` when the stored state is `invalid`. A different broken file or a repair therefore changes the revision.
    - Malformed storage. The view never throws. With an `invalid` stored state, `stored.customAcpCommand` is `null`, and the snapshot and backend messages below name the file and reason. Save accepts a valid command or `null` against that revision. Its "unchanged, no write" shortcut compares against the parsed stored value, and an invalid file never equals it, so save always rewrites the file. Nothing rewrites or deletes the file until the owner saves. After a repair, `stored` shows the new value and `effective` keeps the captured value until restart.
  - UI. The host execution section in `settings/SettingsPanel.tsx` adds one textarea, `自定义 ACP 命令`, with one argv element per line. An empty textarea saves `null`. No other UI is added.
  - Detection. `DepKind` gains `'host-command'`. `isBackendEntryInstalled` reads `customAcpCommand()` and returns true for it when the state is `set` and its first element resolves: `existsSync` for an absolute path, `which.sync` for a bare name. `unset` and `invalid` count as not installed. This feeds `projectCreatableBackends`, `assertBackendUsable`, and the Feishu card picker with no other change. `doctor` is `SessionBackend`'s default, which calls `GenericACPAgentClient.isAvailable` (`generic-acp-agent.ts:75-79`).
  - Registry. The `custom-acp` factory reads `customAcpCommand()` and never the file. If the state is `set`, the factory returns the `SessionBackend` over `new GenericACPAgentClient({ logger, command, providerId: 'custom-acp', label: '自定义 ACP' })`. Otherwise the factory returns a Vonvon backend with the same id, display name, capabilities, and modes. That backend's `isAvailable()` is false, its `doctor()` reports `depState: 'external-missing'` with a hint, its `listThreads` and `readHistory` return empty, and `startThread` and `resumeThread` reject with the same hint. The hint is `未配置自定义 ACP 命令（主机设置 › 执行）` for `unset`, and `自定义 ACP 命令文件无效：<file>（<reason sentence>）。在主机设置 › 执行中重新保存，或修复或删除该文件，然后重启 Vonvon。` for `invalid`. It is the only new backend class. It exists because `createBackend` must return a backend for every catalog id.
  - Snapshot. `readTools` builds the `custom-acp` backend through the registry, so it matches `effective` and never reads the file. Each `createBackend(id).doctor()` probe is isolated as `Promise.resolve().then(() => createBackend(id).doctor({ force: true })).catch(...)`, and a throw maps to that tool's `ToolState` as the existing `claude-agent` catch does (`snapshot.ts:36-42`). With an `invalid` state, the `acp` `ToolState` is `installation: 'unknown'` with `message` set to the invalid hint, which separates it from `missing` for `unset`. `DesktopSnapshot.diagnostics` is not used, because it is keyed per agent (`snapshot.ts:14`, `:86`).
  - Tests. These are required.
    - `resolveCustomAcpCommand` returns `unset`, `set`, and `invalid` with each reason. No `invalid` state, thrown message, or log line contains an argument from the fixture file.
    - Capture. A supervised child gets the parent's state even when the file holds a different command. A timeout or a reply that fails the schema rejects without reading the file. A 64 by 4096 command round-trips through a real `fork` with `'ipc'` stdio.
    - Respawn. The supervisor answers `fcb.custom-acp.req` with the same captured state for a first spawn and for a respawn after the file changed.
    - Corrupt file. Host settings returns without throwing, with `stored` and `effective` `null` and a revision that changes with the file bytes, and save then writes the file. The snapshot shows `acp` as `unknown` with the path and reason. A probe whose `createBackend` throws leaves the other tools' states intact.
- Copilot and OpenCode use the copied default launch, `copilot --acp` and `opencode`, with no runtime settings. `opencode-server` keeps the version gate in *Import closure*. The gate runs in the wrapper's `isAvailable` and `doctor`, and `startThread` and `resumeThread` reject with the 2.0.10 message when it fails. The Feishu card's synchronous install check sees only that the binary exists. The rejection happens before a session starts.

**Build.**
